VCSC PROJECT CONTEXT
====================

Purpose
-------
This is the durable handoff file for continuing the project after a chat
restart. It lives at `.top_secret/context.txt`. When asked to "look at
context.txt and figure out what to do next":

1. Read this file first.
2. Inspect the current tree and verify that it agrees with this record.
3. Continue with the first unfinished item in "Immediate TODO" unless the user
   gives newer instructions.
4. Update this file after every meaningful change or decision.
5. Keep `.top_secret/remove.txt` accurate. It contains one repository-relative path
   per intentionally deleted file, suitable for:

       rm `cat .top_secret/remove.txt`

   Exception: when the user explicitly requests erasing a proper name from the
   project, obsolete paths containing that name are not retained in the ledger.
6. When creating tarballs to hand back, use `date -u "+%Y%m%d_%H%M%S"` to
   generate a filename with a timestamp.
7. Every changelog entry appended to this file must begin with a complete local
   timestamp followed by a comma and the entry title, exactly in this form:

       YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS TZ, entry title

   Generate the timestamp with:

       TZ=America/Los_Angeles date "+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S %Z"

   Never use a date-only changelog heading and never omit the timezone.

Project goal
------------
Maintain VCSC as a brutally pared-down Atari VCS C-like compiler and companion
toolchain aimed specifically at the Atari 2600/VCS. Compatibility with the old
general-purpose N language or n65 tool names is not a goal. Features are changed
in controlled, testable vertical slices.

Current source lineage
----------------------
Initial archive inspected:

    vcsc.20260716_140727.tar.gz

Input archive for this revision:

    vcsc.20260802_191524.tar.gz

The archive has no wrapper directory. COPYING, Makefile, compiler/, test/, and
other top-level entries are directly at archive root. Preserve that layout.

Example hierarchy policy
------------------------
* Public examples are grouped by renderer or display architecture, then by
  layout, then by locally numbered example. Numbers restart in each directory
  and are append-only presentation identifiers rather than permanent global
  IDs. Deleted entries may leave gaps; unrelated groups are not renumbered.
* Current groups:

      examples/01_basic/
      examples/02_faithful_legacy_playercolors/
      examples/03_player_color_192/
      examples/04_player_color_181/
      examples/05_all_five_192/
      examples/06_all_five_181/
      examples/07_player_color_181_unofficial/
      examples/08_all_five_181_unofficial/

* The current tree contains 48 public leaf cartridges: the complete 40-cartridge
  181-line score/gameplay composition matrix; four basic examples; one faithful
  legacy diagnostic; two 192-line player-color examples (interactive motion and
  animated sprites); and one 192-line all-five diagnostic.
* Every leaf directory contains exactly one editable `.c26` cartridge, one
  Makefile, and one README. Renderer and layout directories have READMEs that
  document the shared timing, resource, opcode, and composition contracts.
* Tests must not assume examples are exactly one directory below `examples/`.
  The general build test discovers leaf `.c26` files recursively. Tests that
  validate a specific renderer matrix may keep explicit nested case metadata.
* Source filenames and cartridge stems remain descriptive and are independent
  of local directory numbers.

Settled final-language decisions
--------------------------------
* Target: Atari 2600/VCS using the 6507 CPU.
* All multibyte values are little-endian.
* Binary literals accept visual digits after `0b`/`0B`: `.` is zero and
  `X`/`x` is one. The lexer normalizes them to ordinary binary digits before
  parsing, so all existing integer semantics and range checks remain shared.
* Compiler-owned builtins use the `__builtin_` namespace and ordinary call
  syntax. Registered builtins are compile-time integer expressions, have
  descriptor-owned result type/arity/argument contracts, are shared with
  conditional preprocessing, and emit no runtime call. The first builtin is
  `__builtin_ntsc_rgb(r,g,b)`: three constant `0..255` components, nearest of
  the 128 meaningful even NTSC TIA values by squared RGB distance, lower TIA
  byte on an exact tie, result type `uint8_t`.
* The stock machine interface exposes eight ordinary binary integer types and
  four unsigned packed-decimal integer types:

      int8_t      uint8_t
      int16_t     uint16_t
      int24_t     uint24_t
      int32_t     uint32_t
      bcd8_t      bcd16_t
      bcd24_t     bcd32_t

  The BCD types store two decimal digits per byte in little-endian digit-pair
  order and have numeric ranges 0..99, 0..9999, 0..999999, and 0..99999999.
  BCD `*`, `/`, `%`, `*=`, `/=`, and `%=` are supported only when the decimal
  factor or divisor is a positive integer constant expression equal to `10^n`
  (`1` is `10^0`). Multiplication accepts the constant on either side;
  division and remainder require it on the right. Lowering uses whole-byte
  moves for even powers and four-bit shifts plus `$0f` masks for odd powers,
  never the general multiply/divide runtime helpers and never decimal mode.

* `int`, `char`, and `bool` are ordinary identifiers rather than built-in or
  reserved type names. Programs may introduce them as transparent typedef
  aliases, for example `typedef int16_t int;`.
* String literals are NUL-terminated `uint8_t` arrays stored in cartridge ROM.
  They decay to `uint8_t *` in pointer initializers; writing through such a
  pointer is invalid even though `const` is not yet enforced by the type system.
* No float or double.
* The stock VCS headers expose canonical signed and unsigned 8-, 16-, 24-,
  and 32-bit ordinary types. The compiler core handles user-declared ordinary
  signed or unsigned little-endian integer widths from one through four bytes.
  `bcd32_t` is the largest currently exposed packed-decimal type.
* The stock runtime provides no heap, `sbrk`, `malloc`, or `free`.
* The retained legacy BASIC renderer tree remains source/reference material for
  possible conversion to VCSC; it is intentionally not dead-code pruning
  fodder merely because it is not yet wired into the build. Its exact upstream
  licensing overview remains verbatim in the retained `LICENSE.txt`; project-
  facing names and non-license branding comments are intentionally generic.
* Converted legacy renderers should become source-level VCSC modules included by
  applications. Each module declares and owns the variables and scratch storage
  it needs, and normal compiler/linker allocation should place them unless the
  converted source demonstrably requires a fixed address, alignment, adjacency,
  or page constraint. Describe each renderer or variant with a source-integration
  contract rather than pretending that all retained renderers share one ABI.
* Shared score fonts live under `libraries/vcs/fonts/`, not inside an example.
  Nine 8x8 families are provided as eighteen VCSC modules: one decimal `0-9`
  table and one hexadecimal `0-9A-F` table per family. Every module exports the
  common `score_font` symbol, uses one visual-binary byte per source row, and is
  installed with the VCS support package. Example 03 deliberately selects
  `default_decimal.c26`; this is a settled example choice and must not be
  changed merely to match an older changelog entry.
* void remains a special non-value type.
* Pointers are special 16-bit little-endian values.
* Subtracting compatible pointers yields `int16_t` and a C-style element count.
  Differences outside the representable `int16_t` range are undefined.
* Functions retain normal source-level parameters and lexical scope.
* The final ABI has no software argument/local stack.
* Parameters and automatic locals use statically allocated storage.
* A function has one activation record and is intentionally non-reentrant.
* Direct recursion, mutual recursion, callback re-entry, and any other path that
  activates the same function twice must be rejected.
* Each source-level function name has exactly one signature. Matching prototypes
  and a later definition are legal; ordinary named-function overloading is not.
* JSR/RTS still use the 6502 hardware stack for return addresses.
* Function returns use exact-sized callee-owned zero-page memory:

      void:                         no return object
      8/16/24/32-bit binary integer: function$__return, 1, 2, 3, or 4 bytes
      8/16/24/32-bit packed BCD:    function$__return, 1, 2, 3, or 4 bytes
      pointer:                      function$__return, 2 bytes

  The callee writes `$$`, whose linker-visible symbol is
  `function$__return`, and every value-returning epilogue is plain `RTS`. The
  caller copies directly from the callee object. A, X, and Y are caller-
  clobbered and carry no ordinary language return value.

* All four stock packed-BCD types may be returned. Ordinary binary memory
  returns and stock type names support widths from one through four bytes.
* Struct, union, and array returns remain forbidden even when small. Use an
  explicit result pointer.

Completed vertical slices
-------------------------
1. Value-returning functions own exact-sized zero-page `function$__return`
   objects. Callers consume those objects directly; registers are not part of
   the ordinary return ABI.
2. Every fixed parameter of a directly named function is callee-owned symbol
   storage rather than software-stack storage.
3. Function return types are restricted to void, supported one- through
   four-byte binary and packed-BCD integers, and 16-bit pointers. Aggregates
   and arrays remain forbidden.
4. The VCS linker stopped modeling mirrored $0180-$01FF stack addresses as
   separate physical memory. The first implementation used a fixed $E0-$FF
   reserve; completed slice 24 supersedes that partition with call-graph sizing.
5. Every named automatic local and the hidden `$$` return object now has
   function-qualified fixed-address storage. Automatic initializers still run
   whenever control reaches the declaration on each invocation.
6. The executable `test/software_stack_inventory.pl` regression inventories
   compiler `_pushN`/`_popN` emitters and removed runtime symbols directly from
   the current tree. It detects any reappearance or count drift.
7. Ordinary direct calls evaluate arguments through fixed compiler-generated
   call-site scratch rather than a software-stack call frame. Results are copied
   directly from callee-owned return objects, so return-only scratch is gone.
8. Variadic functions and callable types have been removed. The lexer rejects
   `...`; variadic call lowering, hidden metadata, `stdarg.c26`, `sprintf.c26`, and
   the legacy direct-call software-stack fallback are gone.
9. Function pointers and indirect calls have been removed. Function-pointer
   declarators and function names used as values are rejected; `_callptr0` and
   the final software-stack call frame in `compile_call.c` are gone.
10. General truth-value evaluation and discarded non-call expression results
    now use fixed per-site BSS scratch instead of `_pushN`/`_popN`.
11. Simple assignments into fixed symbols now evaluate and convert through
    fixed per-site `__n65_assigntmp_N` BSS scratch instead of
    `_pushN`/`_popN`.
12. Two-operand comparisons and the main built-in binary operator families now
    use fixed per-site BSS scratch instead of `_pushN`/`_popN`: pointer
    subtraction, scalar/pointer addition and subtraction, shifts, bitwise
    operations, multiplication, division, and remainder.
13. Cast conversion, function-local/global absolute-reference conversion, and
    built-in increment/decrement now use fixed per-site BSS scratch. Fixed
    symbol-to-lvalue helpers cover ordinary, indirect, and bit-field lvalues.
14. Built-in compound assignment, switch-expression comparison, simple
    indirect/absolute/bit-field assignment, runtime local and return-object
    initialization, and local absolute-reference initialization now use fixed
    BSS scratch. `compile_stmt.c` no longer emits `_pushN` or `_popN`.
15. Pointer/index lvalue evaluation, bit-field helper pointer preservation,
    braced assignments, and runtime global initializers now use fixed BSS
    scratch. `compile_lvalue.c` and `compile_init.c` no longer emit `_pushN` or
    `_popN`.
16. Operator overloading, `$exactops`, weak built-in operator dispatch, the
    weak-operator generator/runtime members, and generated exact-operator float
    fixtures have been removed. Compiler source emits no `_pushN` or `_popN`.
    Ordinary named-function overloading was retained temporarily and removed in
    completed slice 31.
17. Floating-point support has been removed from the language, compiler, driver,
    build, runtime, machine definitions, and positive test suite. Floating-point
    literals and `$float`/`$float:*` type flags remain only as direct rejection
    paths.
18. Big-endian and mixed-endian target support has been removed. All target
    integers, pointers, constants, initializers, conversions, comparisons, and
    runtime helpers are little-endian. `$endian:big`, `($big)`, and `($little)`
    remain only as direct rejection paths.
19. [Superseded by slice 39.] Ordinary binary target integer widths were
    temporarily restricted to exactly one or two bytes while inherited wide
    support was pruned. Slice 39 rebuilt and tested width-generic one- through
    four-byte scalar machinery without yet adding the canonical public names.
20. The stock language and machine interface now use only int8_t, uint8_t,
    int16_t, and uint16_t as built-in integer names. Comparisons and logical
    expressions produce an ordinary uint8_t; there is no special boolean type.
21. Simple transparent typedef aliases are supported. `int`, `char`, and `bool`
    are ordinary identifiers and may be introduced as aliases for canonical
    types. String literals used through pointers are emitted in RODATA/ROM.
22. The linker can emit flat `.bin` files, and examples/01_solid_color/solid_color.c26 builds
    and verifies as a complete unbanked 4096-byte VCS cartridge with reset code
    at $F000, valid vectors, the static-frame ABI, and the real shared RIOT RAM
    map.
23. Inline assembly resolves source-level absolute `ref` names according to the
    opcode's operand access: read instructions use the read address, stores use
    the write address, and read-modify-write instructions require identical
    non-`none` read/write addresses. Illegal side use and split-address RMW are
    compile-time errors. The solid-color renderer now uses TIA register names.
24. The VCS linker configuration declares all 128 physical RIOT RAM bytes and
    marks the region `callstack = callgraph`. After archive selection and
    cross-object cycle validation, vcsc-ld computes the longest linked call path,
    reserves two top-of-RAM bytes per function level, and reports/exports the
    resulting hardware-stack range. A configuration may add `callstack_extra`
    bytes for stack use explicitly declared by an included assembly module but
    hidden from source call metadata. Arbitrary inline-assembly pushes, calls,
    and stack-pointer manipulation are still not inferred.
25. Common VCS byte operations now bypass permanent expression scratch.
    Discarded uint8_t increment/decrement, unsigned byte comparisons, constant
    byte stores, and byte-to-hardware-register stores lower directly. A simple
    uint8_t runtime index into a power-of-two-sized array element is scaled
    inline through compiler-owned arg0:arg1 and added to ptr0, with no
    `__vcsc_indextmp_N` or generic multiply helper. The Ode player uses the
    natural `music[music_index].field` form, allocates zero BSS scratch, and
    retains only its two persistent state bytes.
26. All remaining compiler-generated temporary BSS is pooled by function and
    nesting depth as `__vcsc_scratch_N`. Sequential expressions in one function
    reuse a depth slot; nested expressions receive deeper slots; caller and
    callee functions retain distinct physical storage. Leases remain live
    through final copy-out, are released LIFO, and are checked at translation-
    unit completion. Binary expressions whose two operands are fields of the
    same runtime-indexed aggregate element now compute the common address once
    and load both fields by offsets when strict side-effect-free conditions hold.
27. Heap/`sbrk` support has been removed. The `_sbrk` and `__init_sbrk`
    archive members, `_vcsc_sbrk` zero-page pointer, compiler name-mangling
    exception, and allocator documentation are gone. `sbrk` is now an ordinary
    user identifier, and the runtime workspace shrank from 22 to 20 bytes.
28. Explicit runtime `/`, `%`, `/=`, and `%=` operations whose divisor is a
    compile-time positive power of two greater than one emit a performance
    warning. Code generation and C-compatible signed division/remainder
    semantics remain unchanged; literal-only expressions still fold silently.
29. [Helper workspace superseded by slice 44.] The residual target software
    stack was removed. Division/remainder temporarily used a private four-byte
    BSS workspace owned by the archive-selected `_divNle` member.
    `_vcsc_sp`, `_pushN`, `_popN`, `sp2ptr*`, `ARGSTACK`, startup stack-base
    initialization, and the stale per-function frame prologue are gone. Slice
    38 later removed the remaining runtime frame pointer as well.
30. [Superseded by slice 37.] Scalar/pointer return epilogues temporarily used
    direct zero-page loads from the hidden `$$` object. Slice 37 removed those
    loads and made the callee-owned object itself the ABI result. The object
    writer fix for one-byte relocatable operands remains required.
31. Ordinary named-function overloading has been removed. Each source name now
    identifies one declaration/definition signature and one unmangled assembler
    symbol. Matching prototypes remain legal; a second signature is rejected.
    Direct calls retain fixed-parameter count checks, safe integer widening,
    null-pointer literal conversion, typed-object-pointer to `void*` conversion,
    and exact-lvalue enforcement for `ref` parameters. The thousand-line
    overload resolver was replaced by a compact direct-function registry.
32. Generic interrupt-entry support has been removed. The optional `nint`
    archive, compiled `_handle_nmi`/`_handle_irq` defaults, and separate
    `vcsc-rt0-noint.s` member are gone. The stock startup archive member retains
    only weak `__nmi` and `__irqbrk` RTI vector fillers because every 6502 image
    still requires vector addresses. The VCS 6507 has no connected hardware IRQ
    or NMI inputs, and the reduced runtime exposes no compiled interrupt-handler
    ABI.
33. The installed generic 6502 machine definition and the two unwired
    generic linker-layout files have been removed. `test/machine_6502.c26`
    remains only as an isolated compiler-test fixture. Native VCS bindings,
    `vcs_4k.cfg`, and both retained legacy BASIC renderer families are guarded
    by a regression test.
34. The linker's implicit generic machine map has been removed. Direct
    `vcsc-ld` use now requires an explicit script/config, while `vcsc` discovers
    and supplies `vcs_4k.cfg` from the source tree or installed `share/vcs`
    directory when the user does not pass `-T`. Generic end-to-end tests use
    the explicit test-only `test/generic_6502.cfg` layout.
35. Added unsigned packed-decimal `bcd8_t`, `bcd16_t`, and `bcd24_t`.
    `$bcd` is valid only with `$integer:unsigned` on one-, two-, or three-byte
    little-endian scalar declarations. Literal values are range-checked and
    encoded numerically into packed digit pairs, so `42`, `0x2a`, `052`, and
    `0b101010` all store as `$42`. Addition and subtraction use tightly scoped
    `SED`/`CLD` around the actual `ADC`/`SBC` chain; assignment, BCD-width
    conversion, increment/decrement, comparisons, truth tests, fixed parameters,
    and switch are supported. Unsupported arithmetic and runtime BCD/binary
    conversion are rejected. ABI metadata distinguishes BCD from same-sized
    binary integers. `bcd24_t` is returnable through the memory-return ABI.
36. Valued prefix/postfix increment and decrement expressions are no longer
    treated as ordinary reference lvalues by shortcut classifiers. This
    preserves required side effects in compact byte comparisons, direct stores
    to absolute refs, and optimized one-byte array indexes. Ref parameters now
    correctly reject `value++`/`value--` arguments because those expressions are
    values, not lvalues.
37. Function results now remain in each callee's exact-sized hidden zero-page
    `function$__return` object. Non-static definitions export that object;
    separately compiled callers import it when consuming the result. Callers
    copy directly from the return symbol. Value epilogues are plain `RTS`, ABI
    metadata uses `return_memory`, and all four BCD widths return within and
    across translation units.
38. The inherited runtime frame pointer has been removed. Scratch leases now
    select fixed `__vcsc_scratch_N` symbols entirely at compile time, generated
    code addresses those symbols directly, and ordinary calls no longer push or
    restore scratch-base state. `_vcsc_fp`, `fp2ptr*`, startup initialization,
    and the two-byte-per-depth preservation allowance are gone. The stock
    runtime zero-page workspace is 16 bytes, and the linked hardware-stack
    reserve is two bytes per source call depth, plus one fixed two-byte
    startup cursor allowance when runtime initializer functions are linked.

39. Ordinary binary scalar lowering is width-generic from one through four bytes.
    User-declared signed and unsigned little-endian integer types of those widths
    now work through literals, initializers, assignment, extension/truncation,
    parameters, memory returns, arithmetic, comparisons, shifts, bitwise and
    compound operations, increment/decrement, and constant folding. The generic
    multiplication helper already accepted arbitrary widths; division/remainder's
    private dividend workspace was expanded from two to four bytes.
40. Added first-class stock `int24_t` and `uint24_t` declarations. Their
    three-byte little-endian values use the width-generic operator, parameter,
    and memory-return paths. Same-signed widths widen automatically; mixed
    signed/unsigned runtime operations retain the explicit-cast rule. Literal
    encoding preserves VCSC's established width-sized bit-pattern behavior, and
    negative runtime literals now encode correctly instead of reaching the
    unsigned text parser.
41. Added first-class stock `int32_t` and `uint32_t` declarations. Four-byte
    literals, parameters, arithmetic, comparisons, shifts, multiplication,
    division/remainder, and callee-owned memory returns use the already proven
    width-generic paths. Boundary regressions cover exact little-endian layout,
    signed and unsigned limits, fifth-byte rejection, widening, mixed-signedness
    rejection, and cross-object returns.
42. Added first-class stock `bcd32_t` for eight packed decimal digits. Four-byte
    BCD literals encode numeric values as little-endian digit pairs; arithmetic,
    comparisons, widening/truncation, parameters, and memory returns use the
    existing width-generic BCD paths. The legal range is 0..99999999.
43. Completed the retained legacy BASIC standard/multisprite conversion
    inventory without modifying the vendored source. The maintained report
    `libraries/vcs/LEGACY_RENDERER_CONVERSION.md` separates mechanical DASM syntax
    normalization from the real integration work: fixed RIOT RAM ownership,
    hardware-stack use, startup ownership, assembly call metadata, and banked
    linker layouts. It recommends an unbanked 4K standard-renderer profile first,
    with multisprite, Superchip, and bankswitching staged later.
44. Replaced the inherited arbitrary-width multiplication and
    division/remainder helpers with `_mul8`/`16`/`24`/`32` and
    `_div8`/`16`/`24`/`32`. The helpers destructively reuse dead compiler
    expression operands and place exact-width results in the same leased scratch;
    division stores quotient followed by remainder in one adjacent block. The
    private dividend BSS object, `ptr3`, and `tmp0` through `tmp5` are gone.
    Scalar multiply/divide/remainder now stay at the ordinary eight-byte runtime
    workspace baseline.
45. Added whole-program activation overlay. The compiler emits parameters,
    automatic locals, return objects, and pooled expression scratch in
    function-owned activation segments. The linker weights each memory region
    along the complete selected call graph, preserving caller/callee storage
    while overlaying siblings and other mutually exclusive functions. Direct
    calls stage all arguments in caller scratch before touching callee parameter
    symbols, and object-qualified graph identities keep same-named static
    helpers in separate translation units distinct.

Side quest: examples/02_ode_to_joy keeps its 32-step, 128-byte score as a const
ROM array and implements both `music_tick()` and `music_apply_current()` in the
C-like source. The player now uses the natural direct indexed form
`music[music_index].field`; the optimized uint8_t/power-of-two array path emits
no BSS expression scratch and no multiply helper. Only `music_index` and
`music_counter` persist in RAM. The source player runs inside a TIM64T-governed
30-line overscan budget. Two final WSYNCs produce Stella-reported 262-line NTSC
frames; the raw interval between successive VSYNC assertions is 263 whole
scanlines. The execution regression uses that same Stella-equivalent accounting
through ordinary, note-change, silent-gap, and score-wrap paths. The temporary
companion assembly player remains removed.

Current return ABI
------------------
Relevant code:

    compiler/compile_function.c
        return_type_is_void()
        return_type_is_supported()
        return_type_has_value()
        function_has_return_object()
        function_return_symbol_name()
        validate_function_return_type()

    compiler/compile_call.c
        direct-call argument/result handling

    compiler/abi_meta.c
        return_void / return_memory metadata

Every function declaration and definition is validated. The compiler currently
accepts void, one-through-four-byte ordinary binary integers, one-through-four-
byte packed BCD integers, and 16-bit pointers. It rejects aggregates, arrays, and
other noninteger/nonpointer returns. The stock interface names ordinary widths
through 32 bits.

Inside a non-void function, `$$` is the source-level return object. It is an
exact-sized hidden callee-owned zero-page symbol named `function$__return`.
`return expr;` writes it, and the common epilogue is plain `RTS` for every
current width. Non-static definitions `.zpexport` the symbol. A caller in another
object `.zpimport`s it only when the result is consumed.

After `JSR`, the caller copies directly from `function$__return` into the
enclosing destination. Ordinary generated calls do not push or restore compiler
state. A, X,
and Y are ordinary caller-clobbered registers, not result registers. Ignored
return values cause no copy. There is no `return_value` metadata mode, no
caller-owned return slot, and no register-result scratch lease.

Current direct-parameter ABI
----------------------------
Every fixed parameter of a directly named function has a callee-owned symbol.
At each ordinary direct call, the compiler may lease the caller function's
current scratch-depth slot. The lease selects a fixed `__vcsc_scratch_N` symbol
at compile time. The caller:

1. evaluates every argument left-to-right into one caller-owned staging block;
2. after all argument expressions finish, copies the complete converted set to
   the corresponding callee parameter symbols;
3. executes JSR with no compiler-state pushes;
4. when the result is used, copies directly from the callee-owned
   `function$__return` symbol into the enclosing destination.

Scratch is pooled per function and nesting depth. Sequential uses in one
non-reentrant function reuse the same slot and nested uses receive deeper slots.
The compiler emits scratch, parameters, automatic locals, and return objects as
pieces of the owning logical activation. The linker keeps caller/callee paths
disjoint and overlays sibling activations in each physical memory region. Each
slot is emitted once at the maximum size seen for that depth. LIFO release and
an end-of-compilation zero-depth check reject unsafe lifetime overlap. Parameter
symbols may be ordinary absolute storage or zero-page storage.

Call-graph and call-target rules
--------------------------------
No function body may be active twice because its parameters, automatic locals,
and return object share one fixed activation record. Compiler and linker
call-graph checks reject same-unit and cross-object cycles, including cycles
through zero-parameter functions.

Every call target must be a directly named function. Function-pointer
declarators are rejected, as are bare function names or `&function` used as
values. This keeps every call edge visible to cycle analysis and eliminates the
indirect-call ABI and `_callptr0` runtime trampoline.

The same linker-visible function/edge metadata now drives longest-path stack
sizing. The linker counts nodes on the longest acyclic path after all selected
objects and archive members are known. External assembly functions reached by a
source call appear as one graph node, but calls and pushes inside their assembly
bodies are not visible yet.

Current activation overlay
--------------------------
The compiler emits each ordinary function's parameters, automatic locals,
callee-owned return object, and pooled expression scratch in named activation
segments. Static source locals and globals remain ordinary persistent storage.
At link time, every physical memory region is planned independently from the
complete selected call graph:

* a callee begins after the caller bytes still live in that region;
* sibling functions and unrelated roots may share the same physical bytes;
* source-declared `mem` regions retain their configured placement;
* same-named internal functions in different objects receive object-qualified
  graph identities;
* direct calls stage all converted arguments in caller scratch before writing
  any callee parameter symbol.

The overlay changes physical addresses only; source and assembler symbols remain
function-qualified. Inline expansions inherit their enclosing ordinary
function's activation. Runtime global-initializer scratch remains outside the
overlay because calls hidden in its generated startup path are not yet represented
as ordinary source edges.

Atari 2600 hardware constraints
-------------------------------
* The VCS has 128 physical bytes of RIOT RAM at $80-$FF.
* $180-$1FF is a mirror of the same physical RAM, not separate stack RAM.
* Static storage and JSR/RTS/PHA/PHP stack use therefore compete for one pool.
* Every outstanding JSR consumes two physical RAM bytes until RTS.
* The 6507 has no usable IRQ or NMI inputs, so asynchronous interrupt re-entry
  is not the normal problem; program-controlled re-entry still is.
* Visible renderers have only 76 CPU cycles per scanline. Calls in renderers will
  eventually require an explicit policy or automatic inlining.

Current VCS RAM model
---------------------
File:

    libraries/vcs/vcs_4k.cfg

The linker models one physical RIOT RAM pool rather than independent RAM and
CPUSTACK regions. `vcs_4k.cfg` declares the full block:

    $80-$FF   128 physical bytes, with `callstack = callgraph`

Before layout, vcsc-ld shrinks that region from the top. For a maximum linked
source call depth D, the base reserve is `2 * D` bytes: two bytes for each
active JSR return address, including the assembly startup call into a source
root. If the linked image contains one or more runtime initializer functions,
vcsc-ld adds one fixed two-byte allowance because startup preserves its
init-table cursor while calling them. Ordinary generated calls push no
parameter, return, or scratch-base state.

For `01_solid_color`, D=2, so ordinary allocation receives $80-$FB (124 bytes)
and $FC-$FF is reserved. For the fully source-level Ode player, D=3, so ordinary
allocation receives $80-$F9 (122 bytes) and $FA-$FF is reserved. The bytes are
accessed through their page-1 mirrors by the 6502 stack pointer.

The map reports a `CALL STACK` block and the linker exports:

    __call_stack_depth
    __call_stack_extra
    __call_stack_size
    __call_stack_start
    __call_stack_top

A MEMORY region using `callstack = callgraph` may also set
`callstack_extra = N`. The linker adds those explicitly declared bytes to the
source-call and initializer reserve, rejects the option without call-graph
sizing, and reports it in the map. This covers known stack use hidden inside an
included assembly module without inventing a universal fixed stack partition.
The linker still does not infer arbitrary PHA/PHP/JSR or S manipulation from
inline or included assembly; each integration contract must declare its own
extra requirement.

Tests verify dynamic map sizing, a four-level path, overflow enforcement,
explicit hidden-stack reservation, and that the configuration declares the full
RIOT block without a separate CPUSTACK region.

Software-stack status
---------------------
`test/software_stack_inventory.pl` is the authoritative executable inventory.
Compiler source contains zero `_pushN` allocation sites and zero `_popN` cleanup
paths, and the runtime implementation has now been removed completely:

* `_vcsc_sp` is not allocated, exported, or initialized;
* `_pushN`, `_popN`, and every `sp2ptr*` helper are absent from source and the
  runtime archive;
* linker configurations no longer declare an `ARGSTACK` segment;
* compiled functions have no software-stack entry prologue;
* fixed-width multiplication and division/remainder helpers reuse
  compiler-owned expression scratch and select no private BSS or extra runtime
  zero-page cells.

Pooled fixed scratch is selected by a compile-time lease stack and addressed
directly through linker-resolved `__vcsc_scratch_N` symbols. `_vcsc_fp`, its
startup/runtime storage, the `fp2ptr*` helpers, and call-site save/restore code
are gone. The generic regression scans compiler/runtime source, generated code,
and the rebuilt archive so the removed machinery cannot quietly return.

Test policy
-----------
When a feature is removed:

1. delete positive tests whose purpose is to prove that feature works;
2. keep or add negative tests proving the compiler rejects it cleanly;
3. rewrite tests whose real subject remains useful to use legal types or
   out-parameters;
4. list every intentionally deleted path in `.top_secret/remove.txt`.

For the big/mixed-endian purge:

* target multibyte values are unconditionally little-endian;
* the compiler retains only direct rejection paths for `$endian:big`, `($big)`,
  and `($little)`;
* all big-endian and mixed-endian compiler branches and 37 big-endian nlib
  procedures were removed;
* 24 obsolete positive endian tests were intentionally removed and three direct
  rejection tests were added;
* all removed paths are in `.top_secret/remove.txt`.

For the wide-binary-integer purge:

* ordinary binary target integer declarations may be one through four bytes;
* `$bcd` declarations are the deliberate unsigned one- through four-byte
  packed-decimal family;
* target pointers must be two bytes;
* integer literals may carry up to 32 bits when a typed ordinary destination or
  operation supplies a matching width; the stock untyped default remains int16_t;
* positive 24/32/64-bit arithmetic, promotion, extension, shift, helper, and
  return tests were removed or rewritten to legal widths;
* PetiteFFS was removed because its public model requires a 32-bit DWORD;
* dead add/subtract/increment runtime assembly files were removed;
* host-side `long long`, `uint32_t`, and `uint64_t` used inside the compiler,
  assembler, and MD5 implementation are not target-language types and remain.

Files changed in this revision
------------------------------
Hard-renamed tool implementations and build products:

    driver/n65cc.c                -> driver/vcsc.c
    compiler/n65c.c               -> compiler/vcsc_cc1.c
    assembler/n65asm.c            -> assembler/vcsc_as.c
    archiver/n65ar.c              -> archiver/vcsc_ar.c
    linker/n65ld*.c/h             -> linker/vcsc_ld*.c/h
    n65cc/n65c/n65asm/n65ld/...   -> vcsc/vcsc-cc1/vcsc-as/vcsc-ld/...

Hard-renamed runtime and installed layout:

    libraries/nlib/               -> libraries/runtime/
    nlib.l26                      -> libvcsc.l26
    nlib.inc                      -> vcsc-runtime.inc
    nrt0.s                        -> vcsc-rt0.s
    nlib_zeropage.s               -> vcsc-zeropage.s
    /opt/n                        -> /opt/vcsc

All 347 language source, include, example, and test files formerly ending in
`.n` now end in `.c26`. Their 347 old paths, plus 27 other renamed tool/runtime
paths, are recorded individually in `remove.txt`.

Current source/build/documentation files were updated throughout the repository,
including top-level and component Makefiles, driver/tool diagnostics, parser/test
source discovery, ABI/runtime symbol names, archive reader/writer magic, install
rules, examples, Doxygen configuration, and all maintained documentation.

Tests added:

    test/vcsc_branding.pl
    test/vcsc_branding.test

The branding regression verifies the hard-cut paths and suffixes, the renamed
archive signature, the documentation banners, and continued presence of both
legacy BASIC renderer families. The vendored legacy renderer source itself was not
removed or translated. `remove.txt` now contains 861 unique cumulative paths.

Build environment
-----------------
The host lacked bison. Supplied Debian packages were unpacked without root into:

    /mnt/data/vcsc_debroot

Required environment:

    export PATH=/mnt/data/vcsc_debroot/usr/bin:$PATH
    export LD_LIBRARY_PATH=/mnt/data/vcsc_debroot/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu:${LD_LIBRARY_PATH:-}
    export BISON_PKGDATADIR=/mnt/data/vcsc_debroot/usr/share/bison

BISON_PKGDATADIR is required because the privately unpacked bison otherwise
looks under the host /usr/share/bison.

Build and test results
----------------------
Clean warning-as-error tools build:

    make tools -j2

Result: passed.

Compile suite:

    337 passed, 0 failed, 337 total

End-to-end/generic suite:

    202 passed, 0 failed, 202 total

Staged install check:

    passed

All public examples are organized under renderer-specific nested paths and
build as raw 4096-byte cartridges. The general example-build regression discovers
leaf `.c26` files recursively; individual leaf Makefiles compute the correct
repository root from their nesting depth. The staged installed toolchain also
compiles the VCS header smoke program using `int24_t`, `uint24_t`, `int32_t`, and
`uint32_t`. Bare `vcsc-ld` invocation without a script still fails with a focused
diagnostic, while retained generic e2e tests use `test/generic_6502.cfg`.

                  small timing-sensitive unit, try all useful low-byte placements;
                  for ordinary code, use deterministic greedy placement plus
                  bounded local swaps/moves. Minimize taken branch page crossings
                  first, then padding/image growth. Keep the search deliberately
                  bounded for short, reliable sessions.

        [x] 20m. Add indexed-access range metadata for timing-sensitive table
                  reads. Allow a declaration or renderer contract to state the
                  largest legal index and require every effective address in that
                  range to remain on one page. Infer the full declared object range
                  when that is safe; require explicit metadata for pointer-based or
                  deliberately partial ranges.

        [x] 20n. Add hard NMOS 6502/6507 page-wrap hazard checks. Reject or
                  relocate indirect `JMP` pointer words beginning at `$xxFF`, and
                  prevent two-byte zero-page pointers from beginning at `$FF`.
                  Document intentional zero-page wrap semantics separately so the
                  linker does not confuse a hardware hazard with a requested wrap.

                  `frame_ntsc.c26` should publish the standard NTSC phase sizes
                  and small inline operations equivalent to
                  `vcs_ntsc_vsync()`, `vcs_ntsc_begin_vblank()`,
                  `vcs_ntsc_end_vblank()`, `vcs_ntsc_begin_overscan()`,
                  `vcs_ntsc_end_overscan()`, and a scanline-wait helper. The
                  application `main()` remains the composition scheduler and
                  chooses component order, blank lines, and which callbacks run.
                  Low-level helpers such as `wait_for_vblank_timer()` should stay
                  private implementation details rather than becoming an API
                  that lets callers omit the required transition sequence.

                  The scheduler must assert VBLANK and start the shared VBLANK
                  deadline before invoking any `TEMPLATE_vblank()` function;
                  callbacks share the remaining budget and must not own VBLANK,
                  WSYNC, TIM1T/TIM8T/TIM64T/T1024T, INTIM, or TIMINT. The public
                  end-VBLANK operation must distinguish expiry from remaining
                  time, wait only for the unused remainder, issue the final WSYNC,
                  and clear VBLANK at the beginning of the visible scanline. Apply
                  the same scheduler-owned deadline model to overscan. A production
                  overrun continues with one long frame; optional diagnostics set
                  sticky phase bits rather than returning mandatory status.

                  Cover exact 3/37/192/30-line NTSC phase accounting, a stable

                  the shared budget, exact-boundary completion, deliberate

Inline assembly absolute-ref rules
----------------------------------
Inline assembly remains single-line and mostly opaque, but source-level
absolute `ref` identifiers in opcode operands are resolved by the compiler.
Official 6502 loads, compares, and arithmetic reads use the ref read address;
stores use the write address; ASL/LSR/ROL/ROR/INC/DEC and recognized unofficial
read-modify-write opcodes require both addresses to exist and normalize to the
same assembler expression.

The compiler rejects:

* a read instruction using a write-only `@[none/write]` ref;
* a store using a read-only `@[read/none]` ref;
* any read-modify-write instruction using a missing or differing side;
* immediate/control-address use of a ref without one identical read/write
  address;
* a ref operand under an opcode whose access behavior is unknown.

Assembler-local `@labels` are never treated as source identifiers. Inline asm
still does not track register or flag clobbers.

Stop-the-line timing/audio repair
----------------------------------
Before task 20e, examples 02, 03, and 05 received dedicated emulator-backed
regression passes. Example 02 now begins silent and starts its first note during
the first synchronized overscan. Every score transition first writes zero to
AUDV0, then changes AUDC0/AUDF0, and enables the new volume last; retuning an
active note was the source of the recurring transition chirps.

The shared six-glyph display anchors setup to a fresh scanline and flushes both
delayed player-graphics pipelines with `GRP0`, `GRP1`, `GRP0`. More importantly,
the stock 4K profiles now page-align ordinary RODATA: every indexed font read in
the visible renderer must remain on one page or a conditional page-cross cycle can
corrupt the top score row, notably when the rightmost digit is `1`.

Example 05 uses reflected playfield mode and a page-stable standard-renderer hot
loop whose repeated PF stores occur at cycles 24, 31, 38, and 45. The static
scene visibly exercises P0, P1, M0, M1, and BL; player art is stored through the
`VCS_STANDARD_SPRITE_GLYPH` reversal helper. Immutable timing-sensitive
playfields currently use temporary page-boundary companion-source alignment.
Tasks 20e through 20s replace that source-level placement workaround with
linker-understood page constraints, branch-aware placement, NMOS wrap-hazard
checks, and a legal-opcode standard renderer. Stella verifies stable 262-line NTSC
output, correct score rows, an upright target, visible paddle/projectiles, and a
centered untorn playfield. Tasks 20t and 20u completed source-suffix cleanup and cartridge-usage
reporting. Task 20v now supplies one optional stack-safe end-of-frame hook after
persistent state restoration and VBLANK assertion; hook changes affect the next
frame.

A full source-tree hygiene audit removed dead one-off scripts, exact duplicate
fixtures/tests, obsolete simulator-config copies, an empty superseded
unofficial-opcode inventory, and accidental internal residue. The runtime
`unbundle.pl` remains required, and the standard-renderer timing TSV remains an
active reproducible regression artifact. Three previously stranded `.c26`
tests are now executed, generated `coverage_map.h` is no longer stored as
source, and `test/source_tree_hygiene.test` guards against recurrence.


Task 21 now supplies a second maintained standard-renderer profile under
`renderers/standard_4k_ntsc_playercolors/`. It retains P0, P1, BL, the ROM
playfield, and the six-glyph score, restores distinct P0/P1 colors per logical
sprite row, and deliberately omits M0/M1. Static and 320-frame motion examples
lock exact color writes, object rasters, horizontal positioning, frame period,
RAM/ROM/stack costs, normalization, and installed-toolchain use. The user's
`.top_secret/instruction.txt` is intentional durable workflow guidance and must
be preserved.

The compiler assembly peephole pass is enabled by default and can now be
disabled globally with `-fno-peephole` (`-fpeephole` re-enables it). Disabling
the pass preserves candidate generated-assembly patterns while still stripping
internal inline-assembly delimiters. A canonical rewrite-kind catalog is covered
at the optimizer-fragment level, current source-emitted patterns are verified in
both disabled and enabled compiler output, and source inline assembly is opaque
and a state barrier in both modes.

Open implementation questions
-----------------------------
The legacy BASIC tree is intentionally retained for later conversion work.
Functional reduction task 16 and the hard VCSC rename in task 17 are complete.
Tasks 18a through 18f and the task-19 legacy inventory are complete.
Tasks 20a through 20v and task 21 are complete. Runtime-workspace side-quest
items a through d are complete. Task 20q removed all eleven `DCP` sites, task
20r replaced the final source-level `ASR`, `SBX`, and unofficial delay-NOP sites
with legal schedules, task 20s enforced official linked bytes, task 20t made
`.s26` the canonical maintained assembler-source suffix, task 20u added final-
output cartridge-ROM usage reporting, and task 20v added the first stack-safe
gameplay integration boundary. Task 21 added the separate P0+P1+BL per-logical-
row player-color profile with no missiles.

Task 22 and all of its correctness, composition, and public-matrix gates are
complete. Task 23 is the default next unfinished roadmap item and remains the
explicitly optional all-five-object player-color profile. The user may direct
self-contained later work out of order; task 34, the public animated-sprite
example, was completed in that manner without advancing task 23. Tasks 24 and 25
add widely spaced and left/right dual-score six-glyph display variants. Task 26
records the resulting renderer- and score-display-authoring process as a HOWTO.
The former task 20w multisprite placeholder has been removed from task 20 and
expanded into tasks 27 through 30: multisprite contract and normalization,
minimal unbanked integration, general 8K+ bankswitching, and Superchip support.
Task 31 adds measured fixed-point inlining of single-call-site internal functions.
Tasks 32 and 33 add native PAL and SECAM target families. Tasks 35 through 43 add
game examples, paddle/keypad/driving-controller support, a diagnostic cartridge,
playfield colors, and a side-scroller/platform example.

* Whether later to infer PHA/PHP/JSR and S manipulation in inline or included
  assembly. Current integrations declare known hidden use explicitly through
  linker `callstack_extra`; no automatic assembly analysis is attempted.
* Whether common computed-address reuse should later extend across statement
  boundaries. The current implementation deliberately reuses only two operands
  inside one binary expression, where identical index trees and intervening
  clobbers can be ruled out mechanically.
* Whether to add explicit runtime conversion operations between packed BCD and
  ordinary binary integers. Current casts deliberately reject representation
  changes; compile-time constants convert numerically.
* Whether later demand justifies BCD multiplication/division helpers. The
  current hardware-backed feature deliberately supports only add/subtract and
  operations that preserve valid packed digits.
* Whether separate compilation remains throughout simplification.

Change log
----------
Entries are oldest first. Append each new revision at the bottom. Timestamps use
America/Los_Angeles local time and the format `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS PDT`.
Backfilled timestamps are the `context.txt` modification times stored in the
corresponding released archives, so they mark revision completion closely and
reproducibly rather than the exact instant of each individual source edit.

2026-07-16 14:18:32 PDT, initial context
* Documented the static-frame VCS goal and mirrored-RAM problem.
* Added top-level context.txt and remove.txt policy.

2026-07-16 14:53:31 PDT, A:X return slice
* Changed one- and two-byte scalar/pointer returns to A:X.
* Made `$$` a callee-local return scratch object.
* Added return_ax metadata and simulator coverage.
* Temporarily retained legacy oversized/aggregate return storage.


2026-07-16 15:28:56 PDT, default direct-parameter symbols
* Made direct fixed parameters symbol-backed by default.
* Removed fixed direct parameters from software-stack call frames.
* Expanded recursion/cycle validation.
* Rejected parameterized function pointers; retained zero-argument pointers.


2026-07-16 15:58:23 PDT, legal-return enforcement
* Recorded and enforced the two-byte maximum and aggregate-return prohibition.
* Added declaration/definition validation and indirect-call validation.
* Deleted the caller-owned return-slot call-frame path.
* Replaced `return_value` ABI metadata with only return_void or return_ax.
* Renamed user diagnostics from return "slot" to return "object".
* Added negative tests for aggregate, array, float, wide, and big-endian returns.
* Added a positive pointer-to-aggregate A:X test.
* Deleted 35 obsolete positive tests; all paths are in remove.txt.

2026-07-17 15:31:37 PDT, lifetime-pooled scratch and common indexed address reuse
* Replaced the per-site temporary-symbol families with depth-indexed
  `__n65_scratch_N` pools scoped to each function/translation-unit context.
  Sequential expressions reuse a slot, nested expressions receive deeper slots,
  and caller/callee functions retain separate physical storage.
* Added explicit acquire/activate/deactivate/release leases, LIFO validation, and
  an end-of-compilation check that rejects unreleased scratch. Each pooled slot
  is emitted once at its maximum observed size.
* Kept scratch leases live until final copy-out. Execution testing caught and
  fixed an early-release bug where recomputing a computed destination address
  could overwrite the pending RHS value; the same lifetime rule was applied to
  cast, operator, braced, compound, and increment/decrement copy-out paths.
* Added safe common-address lowering for two binary operands that are direct
  fields of the same runtime-indexed aggregate element. Identical index trees
  compute ptr0 once and load both fields by offsets; differing indices and
  ambiguous pointer/bitfield/ref/conversion cases retain independent lowering.
* Added layout, address-count, and execution regressions covering sequential
  reuse, nested separation, caller/callee isolation, computed assignments,
  braced and compound stores, increment/decrement, and shared indexed fields.
* Passed 223/223 compile tests and 94/94 end-to-end tests; both examples remain
  exact 4096-byte cartridges, and Ode passes 1497 consecutive Stella-reported
  262-line frames.
* No files were removed; remove.txt remains at 462 unique cumulative paths.

2026-07-18 16:01:27 PDT, shorten scalar return epilogues for VCS zero page

  the common epilogue to direct loads: `LDA zp; RTS` for 8-bit values and
  `LDA zp; LDX zp+1; RTS` for 16-bit integers and pointers.
* Removed the old indexed epilogue's `LDY` and `TAX` instructions. Including
  RTS, the 8-bit path fell from 6 bytes/12 cycles to 3 bytes/9 cycles, and the
  16-bit path fell from 12 bytes/20 cycles to 5 bytes/12 cycles.
* Fixed the assembler object writer so a plain relocatable expression used by a
  one-byte operand is emitted as a low-byte relocation rather than an invalid
  word relocation.
* Added linked VCS-byte regression coverage for the exact `A5 zp 60` and
  `A5 zp A6 zp+1 60` encodings; updated source-code generation expectations.
* Passed 227/227 compile tests and 96/96 end-to-end/generic tests; both examples
  remain exact 4096-byte cartridges.
* Added two test files and removed none; remove.txt remains at 464 unique
  cumulative paths.

2026-07-19 07:11:04 PDT, add packed-BCD integer family
* Added `$bcd` as an unsigned one-, two-, or three-byte scalar type flag and
  defined stock `bcd8_t`, `bcd16_t`, and `bcd24_t` types for two-, four-, and
  six-digit packed decimal values. The three-byte type is a deliberate
  Atari-specific decimal exception, not restored generic 24-bit binary math.
* Added one numeric literal encoder for static initializers, automatic
  initializers, runtime assignments, aggregate/array elements, typed constants,
  and fixed arguments. Decimal `42`, hexadecimal `0x2a`, octal `052`, and binary
  `0b101010` all become packed byte `$42`; multibyte values use least-significant
  decimal pairs first. Range diagnostics enforce 0..99, 0..9999, and 0..999999.
* Added tightly scoped decimal-mode lowering for BCD `ADC`/`SBC` chains,
  including `+`, `-`, `+=`, `-=`, and valued/discarded `++`/`--`. Generated code
  executes `CLD` immediately after each operation; an execution regression
  verifies the following ordinary binary addition remains binary.
* Added BCD-aware assignment, widening/truncation, comparisons, truth tests,
  switch lowering, parameters, `bcd8_t`/`bcd16_t` returns, and ABI fingerprints.
  Rejected signed/pointer/invalid-width BCD declarations, BCD bitfields,
  multiply/divide/remainder, bitwise operations, shifts, unary minus, runtime
  BCD/binary representation changes, and `bcd24_t` returns.
* Fixed the inherited direct-copy assignment shortcut so `old := value++` and
  its decrement/prefix variants preserve the source side effect; added an
  ordinary-binary end-to-end regression in addition to BCD coverage.
* Added 27 runnable BCD-specific tests plus the generic inc/dec assignment
  regression, and extended the native VCS header smoke test to exercise the
  stock BCD declaration. Passed 250/250 compile tests, 106/106 end-to-end and
  generic tests, the staged install check, both exact 4096-byte cartridge
  regressions, and an installed-toolchain BCD cartridge smoke build.
* Documented representation, literal semantics, supported operations, decimal
  mode invariants, conversion boundaries, and the A:X return limitation. No
  files were removed; remove.txt remains at 487 cumulative paths, and the
  retained legacy BASIC trees were untouched.


2026-07-19 07:40:01 PDT, preserve valued increment/decrement side effects in all shortcut paths
* Confirmed the ordinary assignment bug fixed during BCD work was not the only
  inherited variant. Compact one-byte comparisons could compare `value++`
  correctly while silently skipping the increment; direct byte copies into an
  absolute ref and the optimized one-byte array-index path could do the same.
* Changed reference-lvalue classification so valued prefix/postfix increment and
  decrement expressions are not considered lvalues. This centrally forces
  side-effecting expressions through normal lowering instead of direct-copy or
  direct-index shortcuts.

  parameter calls reject `value++`/`value--` as non-lvalue arguments.

  forms across globals, zero page, locals, function statics, 8/16-bit values,

2026-07-19 11:55:11 PDT, replace register returns with callee-owned memory
* Replaced the A:X scalar/pointer return ABI with exact-sized hidden
  `function$__return` zero-page objects owned by each value-returning function.
  One- and two-byte binary values, pointers, and one-, two-, or three-byte BCD
  values now use the same memory-backed mechanism.
* Changed value epilogues to plain `RTS`; callers restore the frame pointer
  normally and consume the callee return object directly instead of preserving,
  spilling, or reconstructing A:X. Ignored results do not import or copy the
  hidden object.
* Exported non-static return objects and imported them only when an external
  call's value is consumed, so separately compiled functions follow the same
  ABI without creating unnecessary cross-object references. Static functions
  retain private return objects.
* Changed ABI metadata from `return_ax` to `return_memory` and updated compiler,
  runtime, and ABI documentation. Added same-translation-unit and
  cross-translation-unit `bcd24_t` return execution tests, plus code-generation
  tests for static, external, ignored, one-byte, and two-byte return cases.
* Added scalar-width roadmap task 18 before the retained legacy BASIC conversion
  inventory: generalize one- through four-byte scalar machinery, then add
  `int24_t`/`uint24_t`, `int32_t`/`uint32_t`, and `bcd32_t`. Marked memory returns
  complete as task 18a and left legacy renderer conversion as task 19.
* Updated the main README to identify VCSC as the Atari-focused child of the N
  parent project and link `https://github.com/GorillaSapiens/n`.
* Passed 254/254 compile tests, 110/110 end-to-end/generic tests, both exact
  4096-byte cartridge regressions, the staged installation check, and an
  installed-toolchain 4096-byte cartridge using a returned `bcd24_t`. Added six
  superseded test paths to `remove.txt`, which now contains 867 unique cumulative
  paths. The retained legacy BASIC trees were untouched.

  implementation. The compiler emitted the requested table values and correct
  TIA register addresses; the defect was in the player protocol itself.
* Fixed music_apply_current() to write AUDV0=0 before changing AUDC0 or AUDF0,
  then write the new step volume last. Previously every sounding-note-to-gap
  transition changed AUDF0 to C4 while the old volume was still 8 for about 70
  CPU cycles, producing a short wrong-pitch chirp despite the source comment
  claiming that retuning occurred while silent.

  channel-0 volume and rejects any AUDC0/AUDF0 write while that volume is
  nonzero. The predecessor ROM fails this gate; the repaired ROM passes 1497
  complete 262-line frames.

  proves that the existing VCSC retained-source audit is not yet a gold

  frame rather than 20,140/265, and the first visible TIA mismatch is HMM0
  `$60` versus `$70`. The cause is stock upstream BASIC's selected-profile RAM alias
  `player0colorstore = missile0x`, which the current VCSC baseline separated.

  wording from "raw retained renderer" to "retained-source audit" and document
  that example 05 remains provisional until the template matches the pristine

2026-07-26 12:25:45 PDT, add linker-enforced branch page annotations
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Add relative-branch opcode suffixes `.flex`, `.same`, and `.cross`. A bare
  branch and explicit `.flex` are equivalent: placement remains flexible and
  the linker prefers a same-page taken branch. `.same` and `.cross` are hard
  timing contracts. The syntax works on named conditional branches and matching
  raw relative opcode spellings; hard contracts must remain short branches.
* Extend the object branch table from `B26\1` to backward-compatible `B26\2`
  with one page-policy byte per record. The linker continues accepting `B26\1`
  as all-flexible metadata, filters placements that violate hard contracts, and
  minimizes crossings only among flexible branches before its existing growth,
  page, and address tie-breakers. Maps now report both `taken-page=` and
  `policy=` for every retained branch.
* Reject hard annotations whose target lies outside the source's movable code
  layout, long-branch expansions carrying `.same`/`.cross`, invalid suffix use
  on non-relative instructions, and layouts for which no satisfying page phase
  exists. Keep `.flex` eligible for ordinary long-branch relaxation.
* Annotate all twelve timing-critical branches in the faithful retained bB
  player-color renderer and its independent retained-source audit: eleven are
  `.same`, and the terminal visible-loop `BMI` is `.cross`. Remove the old
  `.align 256, $83, $ea` source hack and the RENDERER_CODE 256-byte alignment;
  the linker now chooses the satisfying low-byte phase from the stated timing
  contracts.
* Add `linker_branch_annotations.test` covering named and raw `.flex`, required
  same/cross placement, invalid nonbranch use, prohibited long hard branches,
  an unsatisfiable crossing, and a hard target outside its movable layout.
  Update branch-metadata/page-containment readers and the faithful-renderer test
  to lock eleven satisfied same-page contracts plus exactly one crossing.
* Update the root, assembler, linker, template-design, profile, and example-05
  documentation. Preserve positive equivalence with the pristine bB 1.9 oracle:
  1,230 visible TIA events, 42 stable frames, and 20,064 cycles/264 raw lines.
* Verify 309/309 compile-only tests. The end-to-end runner passed its first 165
  tests before the execution window ended; the explicit remaining eighteen then
  passed 18/18, covering all 183 end-to-end tests with no failures. No files are
  removed. Leave roadmap task 22i4b1a next.

  argument must be a compile-time integer in `0..255`; the result is a

  squared RGB distance, and choose the lower TIA byte on an exact tie. The

2026-07-28 21:22:49 PDT, lower BCD decimal-power multiplication division and remainder inline
* Added packed-BCD `*`, `/`, `%`, `*=`, `/=`, and `%=` support for positive
  integer constant expressions exactly equal to powers of ten across `bcd8_t`,
  `bcd16_t`, `bcd24_t`, and `bcd32_t`. Multiplication also accepts the constant
  on the left; division and remainder keep the conventional constant-right
  form. Other BCD multiply/divide/remainder operands remain rejected.
* Added a shared decimal-power classifier and inline packed-digit lowering.
  Even decimal shifts become byte moves and clears. Odd shifts use four `ASL`
  or `LSR` instructions with `$0f` masks and nibble joins. No `_mul8`..`_mul32`
  or `_div8`..`_div32` helper is selected, and generated code does not execute
  `SED` for these operations.
* Defined fixed-width behavior explicitly: multiplication drops high digits,
  division drops low digits, and remainder retains the low N decimal digits.
  Powers at least as wide as the type therefore produce zero for multiply and
  divide, while modulo returns the original value. `10^0` acts as identity for
  multiply/divide and produces zero remainder.
* Added `test/bcd_power_of_ten_codegen_test.c26` and
  `test/e2e_bcd_power_of_ten_verify.c26`, covering every stock BCD width,
  odd/even powers, reversed multiplication, constant-expression powers,
  static folding, overflow/truncation boundaries, and compound assignment.
  Added focused non-power rejection tests for division, remainder, and compound
  multiplication. Updated compiler/ABI/test documentation.
* Passed 321/321 compile tests. The end-to-end/generic suite passed all 191
  tests; the first 170 were observed passing before the runner command hit the
  execution ceiling, and the remaining 21 were rerun explicitly and passed.
  No files were removed; `.top_secret/remove.txt` is unchanged.

2026-07-28 22:22:07 PDT, add cheap integer and packed-BCD constant arithmetic optimizations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Add general fixed-width integer lowering for multiplication by zero or one,
  division by one, remainder by one, and division/remainder by a positive
  constant larger than every possible left-operand value. Apply the same rules
  to compound assignments while evaluating operands and lvalues exactly once.
* Add packed-BCD remainder by two and five using only low-digit masks and one
  conditional subtract. Add multiplication by constants expressible as
  `10^a + 10^b` or `10^a - 10^b`, provided the multiplier fits the BCD type;
  form two decimal-shifted copies and combine them with one scoped decimal add
  or subtract instead of selecting a general multiplication helper.
* Fuse chains of packed-BCD multiplication, division, and remainder by powers
  of ten into one contiguous digit-window copy. Also fuse
  `x - (x % 10^n)` for ordinary variables while deliberately retaining both
  evaluations for calls, indirect expressions, and hardware references.
* Keep unsupported general BCD multiplication, division, and remainder
  rejected. Add an explicit rejection boundary for cheap-form multipliers that
  do not fit the destination BCD type, such as `bcd8_t *= 101`.
* Add compile-time assembly-shape checks and simulator verification covering
  zero/one identities, signed and unsigned oversized-divisor boundaries,
  side-effect preservation, all four BCD widths, `% 2`, `% 5`, decimal

2026-07-29 20:12:44 PDT, add the lone-underscore discard token
------------------------------------------------------------
* Have the lexer return a dedicated `DISCARD` token for the exact one-character
  spelling `_`; longer identifiers containing underscores remain ordinary
  identifiers.
* Add value-less simple-assignment forms in both directions. `lvalue := _`
  performs no source load or conversion and stores whatever byte is already in
  A; a direct one-byte absolute register such as `WSYNC` therefore lowers to
  one bare `STA`. `_ := expression` evaluates the expression normally for
  calls, assignments, and other effects without creating a destination object.
* Restrict assignment from discard to one-byte, non-bitfield lvalues. Reject
  wider and bitfield targets rather than inventing ambiguous multibyte or
  read-modify-write semantics. Both discard forms are intended as value-less
  expression statements rather than operands in larger expressions.
* Add direct-codegen, lexer-distinction, rejection, parser-coverage, and generic
  simulator regressions. Update the root language summary, compiler reference,
  and test documentation.
* No files were removed; `.top_secret/remove.txt` is unchanged.

  fixed scratch object for the whole chain instead of allocating and copying
  through one scratch object per assignment level.
* Add an accumulator-only fast path for discarded chains whose targets and
  source are directly addressable one-byte values. Constants are loaded once
  into A and direct lvalue sources are loaded once; each following STA preserves
  A for the next target. The reported TIA chains now contain no compiler scratch,
  no Y setup, and no destination readback.
* Add shared direct-byte lvalue load/store helpers and a preserved-symbol-to-
  lvalue copy helper so assignment lowering uses direct fixed-symbol accesses
  rather than unnecessary ptr0/ptr1 setup.
* Tighten the write-only TIA chain regression to reject all compiler scratch and
  Y instructions. Add a wider three-target regression that requires one shared
  scratch object and rejects nested scratch and pointer setup. Update compiler
  and test documentation for both lowering paths.
* No files were removed; `.top_secret/remove.txt` is unchanged.
* Verification: 334/334 compile-only tests pass. All 197 end-to-end/generic
  tests are accounted for and pass; the combined run completed 174 before its
  outer command ceiling and the remaining 23 passed explicitly. Staged
  `make installcheck` also passes.

2026-07-31 07:55:12 PDT, repair alternating all-five playfield phase
------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Diagnose the shredded playfield in every all-five renderer as a two-cycle
  phase mismatch inside the steady two-scanline loop. The P1 half decremented Y
  before writing the reflected PF2/PF1 pair, placing those writes at cycles
  47/54, while the following P0 half wrote them at 45/52. The alternating phase
  visibly tore the right half even though the old byte-level raster oracle
  happened to accept it.

  including `WSYNC := RESP1 := RESP0 := _;`. The compiler now preserves the
  accumulator value already present on entry and emits one-byte stores from the
  innermost target outward, so the example writes RESP0, then RESP1, then WSYNC
  without reading a write-only TIA register or manufacturing a source value.

  non-bitfield lvalues. This guarantees that every store preserves A without
  hidden compiler-stack pushes or temporary-value scratch. Add focused ordered-

2026-08-01 13:45:00 PDT, refine common trampolines and bank placement policy
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Replace the earlier per-target paired-JSR bridge plan in
  `.top_secret/bankswitching.txt` with one byte-identical trampoline table at the
  same physical offset in every bank. A direct cross-bank call now reaches a
  generated target entry, records the ordinary 16-bit target, pushes a synthetic
  source-bank return stub, selects the destination bank, and enters the callee
  with `JMP (target)`. The callee's RTS reaches the identical return stub, which
  restores the caller's bank before the original call-site RTS completes.
* Apply the same target-pointer/destination-selection machinery to one-way JMP
  transfers without the synthetic return. Keep programmer-authored indirect
  calls unsupported; the indirect JMP is internal linker/runtime machinery.
  Retain the two-byte weighted hardware-stack charge for each active cross-bank
  call and require target entries, source entries, return stubs, and dispatcher
  bytes to be replicated identically in every physical bank.
* Choose constrained automatic placement rather than either all-manual placement
  or a call-graph-only guess. Pin `main`, startup, and mandatory runtime material
  to BANK0; allow programmers to pin functions, ROM objects, translation units,
  or same-bank groups; collapse forbidden cross-bank ROM-data edges into hard
  placement components; and automatically place unpinned components with a
  deterministic call-graph-aware greedy policy. Reserve later roadmap work for
  measured heuristic improvements rather than making automatic packing optional.
* Strengthen the internal source-tree hygiene guard and test-harness notes to
  retain the identical common trampoline, JSR-to-indirect-JMP, `main`-in-BANK0,
  and constrained automatic-placement decisions. This remains design-only and
  changes no compiler, assembler, linker, runtime, cfg, or public documentation.

2026-08-01 14:10:44 PDT, use named mem regions for explicit function-bank placement
---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
* Update `.top_secret/bankswitching.txt` to make the existing named `mem` region
  modifier the sole explicit function-bank spelling. A definition such as
  `bank1 void some_function(void)` now means that the function belongs to the
  previously declared `bank1` region; no `bank0` through `bank7` language
  keywords are added. Unmarked functions remain available for later automatic
  placement, while `main` may be unmarked or explicitly `bank0` and is rejected
  when explicitly assigned to a numbered nonzero bank.
* Complete bankswitching roadmap item 1. The compiler emits marked functions in
  `CODE.<region>`, emits the same region metadata already used by objects,
  requires declarations and definitions to agree, and rejects region modifiers
  on inline functions. The assembler now preserves procedures in `CODE` and
  `CODE.*` as independent private o26 layouts. The linker resolves a private
  name such as `CODE.bank1.__vcsc_function$foo` through the exact `CODE.bank1`
  rule before falling back to `CODE`.
* Clarify Superchip region arithmetic. A source declaration beginning at `$D100`
  must use `$size:0x0F00`, not `$size:0x1000`, because the source `mem` interval
  describes allocatable ROM and must stop at `$DFFF`. The future cartridge image
  model still emits the complete 4K physical bank and separately fills the
  reserved `$D000-$D0FF` RAM-port prefix.

  declaration conflicts, inline misuse, and nonzero-bank `main`. Add an

  `CODE.bank1.__vcsc_function$placed` resolves at `$D100` under a

2026-08-02 10:59:18 PDT, repair stale frame-pointer-removal stack assertion

  spelling `bytes = (uint32_t)depth * 2u`.  Bankswitching roadmap item 6
  intentionally changed the reservation basis to `weighted_depth`, where every
  active source call contributes one return-address slot and every active
  cross-bank edge contributes one additional trampoline return-address slot.

  the linker's weighted depth to be clamped to at least the ordinary call depth.
